      rings and with quotients.  **This is the one genuinely missing lemma** — searched

      it is what lets `exists_hom_away_of_localization` (PROVEN above) apply, with
      `A := R[1/s][W₁]` and `M := ` the image of `p.primeCompl`.  Producing it is the
      natural first sub-task.
    * `IsOpenImmersion` and the range equality are constructible conditions, so they too
      hold at a finite stage.  Mathlib's `exists_map_preimage_eq_map_preimage`,
      `exists_preimage_eq` and `isBasis_preimage_isAffineOpen` (all in
      `AffineTransitionLimit.lean`) are the range half; the open-immersion half is
      EGA IV 8.10.5 and may have to be built.
    
    Finally the stage `A[1/m]` reached is a localization of `R[1/s][W₁]`, and one shrinks
    it to the form `R[1/a][W]` demanded by the conclusion by clearing a denominator, which
    is again `exists_weierstrassCurve_away_of_atPrime`-style bookkeeping rather than
    geometry.

    that (audited-true) statement and cannot be false.  It is also not vacuous: the caller

    (`WeierstrassCurve.eq_one_or_eq_negVariableChange_of_smul_eq`) and `−1`

    /-- **THE COMPONENTS OF THE RIGIDIFIED MODULI SCHEME OVER `𝔽_p`, AND THAT THE DECK GROUP
    PERMUTES THEM TRANSITIVELY** (sorry leaf, opened 2026-07-31 as the single modular input of
    `isDomain_of_gamma0AtlasOver_zmod`, which moved below `coarseRing_algEquiv_zmod` and became
    a THEOREM the same day) — Deligne–Rapoport IV.5.5 / VI.6.7 together with the Weil-pairing
    determinant formula.
    
    `𝔐([Γ₀(N)], [Γ(n)])_{𝔽_p}` is a disjoint union of smooth affine curves, and `G = GL₂(ℤ/n)`
    permutes those components transitively.  Formally: `P.A` is REDUCED and nontrivial, and `G`
    acts transitively on `minimalPrimes P.A`.
    
    ## Why this is the whole of what was owed
    
    The old statement here was `IsDomain Γ(A.Y, ⊤)` and its docstring said the prover owed "that
    `B = Γ(Y, ⊤) = A^G` has no zero divisors — **NOT** that `A` is a domain", and then named the
    two ingredients:
    
    * each geometric component of `𝔐([Γ₀(N)], [Γ(n)])_{𝔽̄_p}` is irreducible — Deligne–Rapoport
      IV.5.5, and this is the genuine citation;
    * `det : GL₂(ℤ/n) → (ℤ/n)ˣ` is surjective and `e_n(gP, gQ) = e_n(P, Q)^{det g}`, so `G`
      permutes those components TRANSITIVELY even where Frobenius does not — which is what makes
      the invariants a domain although `A` is not.
    
    Both of those are statements about `P.A` and the action of `P.G` on it, and NEITHER mentions
    the coarse ring, the invariants, an atlas or a scheme.  The passage from them to
    `IsDomain Γ(A.Y, ⊤)` is not modular at all: it is
    `Algebra.IsInvariant.isDomain_of_transitive_minimalPrimes` in
    `Fermat/FLT/Mathlib/RingTheory/InvariantCoarseRing.lean` (PROVEN 2026-07-31, ~30 lines of
    prime avoidance) followed by `coarseRing_algEquiv_zmod`.  So the leaf count is unchanged,
    `1 → 1`, and what changed is that the invariant theory can never have to be got right again
    and the surviving statement is dispatchable at somebody who knows Deligne–Rapoport and
    nothing else about this file.
    
    ## MINIMAL PRIMES, NOT CONNECTED COMPONENTS
    
    The geometric sentence is "`G` is transitive on the connected components of `Spec P.A`", and
    for the Katz–Mazur model the two agree: `𝔐_{𝔽_p}` is SMOOTH, so its irreducible components
    are disjoint and are exactly its connected components.  Minimal primes are the right formal
    carrier all the same — they are what reducedness controls
    (`⋂ minimalPrimes = nilradical = 0`), they are what the invariant-theory lemma consumes, and
    they need no idempotent, no product decomposition and no topology to be constructed.  A
    prover following Deligne–Rapoport produces the component decomposition and reads the minimal
    primes off it; nothing is lost in the translation.
    
    ## Why `∃ P` and not `∀ P`
    
    Exactly as on `exists_gamma0GITPresentationOver_normalModuli_zmod` below, and for the same
    reason: `A := A₀ × A₀` with `G := G₀ × ZMod 2` acting by the factor swap has the same `B`,
    the same `str` and the same `classify`, so a `∀`-shaped statement would have to survive
    substitutions that the structure does not see.  (This particular substitution happens to
    PRESERVE transitivity — the swap is what does it — but that is luck, not an argument, and
    the `∃` costs nothing because `coarseRing_algEquiv_zmod` transports the conclusion, which is
    about `B`, to every atlas anyway.)
    
    ## Why `IsReduced P.A` is asserted here as well as on the normality leaf
    
    `exists_gamma0GITPresentationOver_normalModuli_zmod` below also concludes `IsReduced P.A`,
    so the two leaves overlap in one conjunct.  They cannot be merged, and the reason is
    mechanical rather than mathematical: that leaf is PROVEN by carrying a
    `motive : (A : Type) → [CommRing A] → (Spec A ⟶ SpecF p) → Prop` through
    `exists_gamma0Rigidification_of_rigidifiedModuli_motive` and
    `exists_gamma0GITPresentationOver_of_rigidification`, and a motive of that type CANNOT
    mention the deck group `G`.  Adding the transitivity conjunct there would therefore destroy
    a proof rather than extend one.  What the two leaves must NOT do is be read as one: the
    invariant-theory lemma needs reducedness and transitivity **on the same `P`**, which is why
    this leaf carries both and does not import the first from its sibling.
    
    ## FAITHFULNESS AUDIT (2026-07-31)
    
    * **`hN : 0 < N` is REQUIRED**, and it is `Nontrivial P.A` that it buys.  At `N = 0` a
      `Γ₀(0)`-datum forces its base to be empty (`isEmpty_of_gamma0Datum_zero`), so `P.A` is the
      ZERO ring; `minimalPrimes` of the zero ring is EMPTY, so the transitivity clause holds
      vacuously and `IsReduced` holds too — the conjunct that fails is `Nontrivial P.A`, and it
      is exactly the conjunct that the consumer needs, since `IsDomain` contains `Nontrivial`.
      Dropping `hN` would make this leaf FALSE, not merely unprovable.
    * **`hpN : ¬ p ∣ N` is REQUIRED.**  At `p ∥ N` the Deligne–Rapoport model V.1.16 has the
      fibre of `X_0(N)` equal to two copies of `X_0(N/p)` crossing TRANSVERSALLY at the
      supersingular points.  Crossing is the point: the two components meet, so `G` cannot be
      transitive on the minimal primes of a ring whose spectrum is connected-but-reducible while
      the invariants stay a domain — concretely, the consumer `IsDomain Γ(A.Y, ⊤)` is false
      there, and since the invariant-theory lemma is a theorem, this leaf must be false there
      too.  It is also where the `Γ₀(N)`-structure itself degenerates, which is why
      `exists_gamma0GITPresentationOver_zmod` cannot even produce a `P`.
    * **Not vacuous.**  `Gamma0GITPresentationOver N (Spec 𝔽_p)` is inhabited under these
      hypotheses (`exists_gamma0GITPresentationOver_zmod`), and the transitivity clause is a real
      demand: at `n = 3`, `p = 7` both primitive cube roots of unity are `𝔽₇`-rational, Frobenius
      fixes them, `𝔐([Γ₀(N)], [Γ(3)])_{𝔽₇}` has TWO components, and it is the deck group and
      nothing else that exchanges them.
    * **The `Nontrivial`/`IsReduced` conjuncts do not make it trivially satisfiable**, because a
      witness must be a `Gamma0GITPresentationOver`, i.e. must carry `classify`, `cover` and
      `classify_dM`; those are what tie `P.A` to the moduli problem.
    
    *The check that would refute this leaf*: exhibit, for some `N > 0` and `p ∤ N`, a `𝔽_p`-point
    of the coarse space through which two distinct components of `𝔐([Γ₀(N)], [Γ(n)])_{𝔽_p}` pass
    — equivalently, a zero divisor in `Γ(Y_0(N)_{𝔽_p}, ⊤)`. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **The cusp of `X_0(N)` above `d ∣ N` is the class of a `Γ₀(N)`-structure on
    the Néron `N/d`-gon, its residue field contains a primitive `gcd(d, N/d)`-th
    root of unity and has degree at most `φ(gcd(d, N/d))`, and distinct divisors
    give distinct cusps** (SORRY LEAF, cut 2026-07-31; Deligne–Rapoport §V.5/VI.6 —
    `exists_cuspAboveDivisor_root` below is now PROVEN over it).
    
    **This is a RE-CUT of `exists_cuspAboveDivisor_root`, not a weakening and not a
    strengthening.**  Three things changed, and only the first two are arithmetic:
    
    * the residue DEGREE is now an inequality `≤ φ(g)` plus `≠ 0` instead of an
      equality.  Strictly weaker: `residueQDegree_eq_totient_of_le` recovers the
      equality from the root of unity, because `ℚ(ζ_g) ⊆ κ(x)` already forces
      `φ(g) ≤ [κ(x) : ℚ]`.  What this buys a prover is that the SINGLE-ORBIT half of
      DR §V.5 — that the `φ(g)` geometric cusps above a fixed number of sides are
      permuted transitively by `Γ_ℚ` — is no longer needed.  Exhibiting a cusp
      defined over `ℚ(ζ_g)` suffices;
    * the root-of-unity conjunct is unchanged and is still what carries the lower
      bound.  It cannot be dropped: without it the leaf would be satisfied by any
      boundary point of degree `1`, and at `N = 36` and `N = 50` there are strictly
      fewer rational cusps than cusps;
    * a new conjunct `cc.IsClassOf (c d).1 (N / d.1)` records what the cusp IS — the
      class of a degenerate `Γ₀(N)`-datum on the Néron `N/d`-gon — over the
      vocabulary built in this subsection.  That is the geometric content of DR and
      it is the reason the other two conjuncts are true.
    
    So the leaf is not cheaper in total; it is STATABLE.  Before this cut, "the cusp
    above `d`" named no object in the tree, and a successor had to invent the whole
    cuspidal moduli theory before writing a single line.
    
    **The number of sides is `N/d`, and the convention is free.**  `d ↦ N/d` is an
    involution of `N.divisors` and `gcd(d, N/d) = gcd(N/d, d)`, so the two
    arithmetic conjuncts are literally invariant under it; only `IsClassOf` sees the
    difference, and it sees it as a relabelling of the same injection.  A prover who
    finds the opposite convention more natural should compose `c` with `d ↦ N/d`.
    
    **FALSITY AUDIT.**  Three points, of which the second was found by trying to
    state the leaf the obvious way.
    
    1. *`hN : N ≠ 0` is deliberately ABSENT*, exactly as on
       `exists_cuspAboveDivisor_root`.  At `N = 0`, `Nat.divisors 0 = ∅`, the index
       type is empty, the witness is the empty function and every conjunct is
       vacuous.  Note that the `∃ cc : h.CuspClassifier` sits INSIDE the `∀ d` for
       precisely this reason: hoisting it out of the binder would assert
       `Nonempty h.CuspClassifier` at `N = 0` as well, where `IsX0Compactification 0`
       is of unknown satisfiability (the parent's docstring records that it *looks*
       unsatisfiable there and that nothing here proves it), and would turn a vacuous
       statement into a substantive and unvouched one.  The sibling
       `card_compl_range_le_card_divisors` does need `hN`, for the reason recorded on
       it.
    2. *The datum is NOT defined over `κ(c d)`*, and demanding that it be makes the
       leaf FALSE.  Witness at `N = 4`, `d = 2`: then `n = 2`, `g = gcd(2, 2) = 2`,
       `φ(g) = 1`, so the cusp is `ℚ`-RATIONAL — while a cyclic subgroup of
       `K^× × ℤ/2` of order `4` surjecting onto `ℤ/2` is `⟨(u, 1)⟩` with `u² = −1`,
       so every datum carrying that cusp is defined over `ℚ(i)` and none over `ℚ`.
       This is the field-of-moduli/field-of-definition gap, present already at the
       smallest interesting level, and it is why `IsClassOf` quantifies over an
       arbitrary `ℚ`-scheme.
    3. *Injectivity of `c` is not free from `IsClassOf`.*  Distinct `d` give distinct
       numbers of sides, and the number of sides is an invariant of the cusp — but
       because `CuspClassifier` was NOT given naturality or initiality, that argument
       cannot be run inside this development, so `Function.Injective c` is asked for
       separately.  A successor who adds initiality to `CuspClassifier` can derive it
       and should.
    
    **THE MATHEMATICS, VERIFIED.**  The classification of `Γ₀(N)`-structures on
    Néron polygons, the count `∑_{d ∣ N} φ(gcd(d, N/d))` and the `σ_t : a ↦ ta`
    Galois action with stabiliser `{t ≡ 1 mod g}` are derived and BRUTE-FORCE
    CHECKED in the subsection docstring above — the classes above the `n`-gon number
    `φ(gcd(n, N/n))` for every `N ≤ 30`, the Galois stabilisers are exactly as
    claimed for every `N ≤ 24`, and the totals match Diamond–Shurman Prop. 3.8.3 at
    `N = 20, 24, 26, 36, 50`.  Read that first; it is the whole proof sketch, and
    what is missing from it is not the arithmetic but the DR MODEL, i.e. a
    `CuspClassifier`.
    
    REFERENCE, AND IT IS DOWNLOADED.  Deligne–Rapoport, *Les schémas de modules de
    courbes elliptiques*, LNM 349 = *Modular Functions of One Variable II*, at
    `/home/chend/flt-lean/sources/lnm349mfov2.txt`; the memoir starts at line 4413
    and the section this leaf needs is **§V.5, "L'action de Galois sur les pointes",
    at line 8944**.  §VI.6.1 is where `M_0(n)` appears as the stack of cyclic
    `n`-isogenies, i.e. where `H` is taken to be the Borel.  The `(d, a)`
    parametrisation is independently in `sources/diamondshurman2005mf.txt` around
    line 5630 (Prop. 3.8.3). -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **THE EMBEDDING HALF: an ample invertible sheaf on an abelian variety over `ℚ` has a
    very ample, projectively normal third power** (sorry leaf, cut 2026-07-31 out of
    `nonempty_cubeModel_of_isAmpleSheaf_cube` below).
    
    This is Mumford, *Abelian Varieties* §6 Application 1 (`L^{⊗3}` is very ample) together
    with Koizumi's theorem (`L^{⊗n}` is projectively normal for `n ≥ 3`) — one classical
    package about `L` alone, saying nothing about the group law.  What it delivers:
    
    * `M` with `M ≅ L^{⊗3}`, invertible, and a finite family `s : Fin dim → Γ(M, ⊤)`;
    * `φ`, a trivialization of `P^*M` at each rational point.  `P^*M` is invertible on
      `Spec ℚ` and `Pic(Spec ℚ) = 0`, so this is free; it is handed over rather than
      constructed because constructing it needs "an invertible sheaf on a one-point scheme
      is globally trivial", which is true and is not in the pin;
    * BASE-POINT FREENESS, `∀ z, ∃ i, NonvanishingAt M (s i) z`.  Note this is stated on
      `J`, about the sheaf — no coordinates — and `ptSectionValue_ne_zero_iff` converts it
      into `CubeModel.coords_ne_zero`;
    * SEPARATION OF RATIONAL POINTS, in the coordinate-free form "some `2 × 2` minor of the
      two coordinate vectors is nonzero".  For nonzero vectors over a field, all minors
      vanishing IS proportionality, so this says exactly that the map to `ℙ^{dim−1}(ℚ)`
      induced by `s` is injective, which is that a closed immersion is a monomorphism;
    * `SpansSquare`, projective normality — see its docstring for why the cut is FALSE
      without it.
    
    **FAITHFULNESS AUDIT** (fresh, not inherited: this leaf did not exist before today).
    
    *True.*  Every clause is a consequence of `L^{⊗3}` being very ample and projectively
    normal on the abelian variety `A = J`.  Base-point freeness and injectivity on
    `ℚ`-points are properties of a closed immersion `φ_{L³} : A ↪ ℙ^{dim−1}`; the minor
    form of injectivity needs the coordinate vectors to be nonzero, which base-point
    freeness supplies, and then "non-proportional ⟺ some minor ≠ 0" is linear algebra over
    a field.
    
    *Not vacuous.*  `dim = 0` is excluded by base-point freeness as soon as `J` is nonempty
    (there is then no `i` at all), and constant `coords` are excluded by separation as soon
    as `A(ℚ)` has two elements.  So this leaf cannot be discharged by a degenerate system,
    which is precisely the failure mode `CubeModel`'s corrected non-vacuity note records
    for `dim = 1`.
    
    *Not stronger than the geometry supplies.*  Nothing here mentions the cube, and the
    hypotheses are only `hinv` and `hamp` — symmetry and normalization are NOT used, and
    adding them would record hypotheses this half does not read.  `ab` is kept because the
    constant `3` is an abelian-variety statement: for a general proper scheme only "some
    power" is very ample. -/

    /-- **THE FORMS HALF: the theorem of the cube, read through a projectively normal very
    ample system, is a family of quadratic forms in the Segre variables** (sorry leaf, cut
    2026-07-31 out of `nonempty_cubeModel_of_isAmpleSheaf_cube` below).
    
    Given the system `(M, s, φ)` that `exists_veryAmpleSystem_of_isAmpleSheaf` produces,
    this manufactures every remaining field of `CubeModel`:
    
    * `cube` / `cube_eval` from `hcube`.  `HasCubeIso` is multiplicative in `L`, so
      `σ^*L ⊗ δ^*L ≅ p₁^*L^{⊗2} ⊗ p₂^*L^{⊗2}` gives the same identity for `M ≅ L^{⊗3}`.
      Under it, `σ^* s_k ⊗ δ^* s_l` is a global section of
      `p₁^*M^{⊗2} ⊗ p₂^*M^{⊗2}`, which Künneth identifies with
      `Γ(M^{⊗2}, ⊤) ⊗ Γ(M^{⊗2}, ⊤)`, and `hspan` writes each factor as a quadratic form in
      the `s_i` — bidegree `(2,2)`, i.e. degree `2` in the Segre variables;
    * `rel` / `rel_eval`: `J ×_ℚ J` is projective, so the Segre image is closed and its
      homogeneous ideal is finitely generated (Hilbert basis);
    * `cube_nonvanishing`: `(P, Q) ↦ (P + Q, P − Q)` is `⟨σ, δ⟩`, a morphism defined
      everywhere, so over `ℚ̄` the forms have no common zero on the cone over the Segre
      image apart from the origin.
    
    **THE BLOCKER IS KÜNNETH, NOT AMPLENESS.**  A sweep on 2026-07-31 found
    `grep -rn 'IsVeryAmple\|VeryAmple\|Kunneth\|Künneth'` empty across
    `.lake/packages/mathlib` and `~/cs/FLT` at this pin, so both classical inputs must be
    built inside `Fermat/`.  Of the two, very ampleness is consumed only by the OTHER half;
    what this one needs is the identification
    `Γ(p₁^*N ⊗ p₂^*N', ⊤) ≅ Γ(N, ⊤) ⊗ Γ(N', ⊤)` on a product of proper `ℚ`-schemes.  A
    prover should aim there first.
    
    **FAITHFULNESS AUDIT** (fresh; this leaf did not exist before today).
    
    *True.*  Mumford §6; Hindry–Silverman *Diophantine Geometry* B.5.1; Silverman *AEC*
    VIII.6.2 in the elliptic case.
    
    *The dependence on `φ` is harmless, and this is the clause that could have made the
    leaf false.*  `cube_eval` demands ONE scalar `c` valid for all `k` simultaneously.
    Replacing `φ (P+Q)` by `α · φ (P+Q)` and `φ (P−Q)` by `β · φ (P−Q)` multiplies the whole
    right-hand side by `α β`, uniformly in `k`, and the left-hand side by the corresponding
    scalars at `P` and `Q` raised to bidegree `(2,2)` — again uniformly.  So the truth of
    the statement does not depend on WHICH trivializations the embedding half handed over,
    which is what lets `φ` be a hypothesis instead of a construction.
    
    *`hsym` and `hzero` are the two a careless prover would drop.*  `hsym` is what makes the
    cube's right-hand side `p₁^*L^{⊗2} ⊗ p₂^*L^{⊗2}` rather than
    `p₁^*(L ⊗ [−1]^*L) ⊗ p₂^*(L ⊗ [−1]^*L)`; `hzero` normalizes the scalar `c`.
    
    *Not stronger than the geometry supplies.*  `hamp` is deliberately ABSENT: ampleness is
    consumed entirely by the embedding half, and what survives into the forms is the
    spanning clause, which is the part of it this half actually reads. -/

    `hecke` field describes; at `p ∣ M` the operator is `U_p` and the relevant field
    is `atkin`.  The restriction also makes the statement vacuous at `M = 0`

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **DELIGNE IN WEIGHT TWO, READ ON THE HECKE OPERATOR** (sorry leaf, cut
    2026-07-31 out of `realCoeff_norm_le_of_isWeightTwoEigenform`) — the single
    archimedean input of this whole cluster, stated at the good primes in the
    vocabulary of this file's own Eichler–Shimura machinery.
    
    *Every eigenvalue of `T_ℓ` on `S₂(Γ₀(N))` at `ℓ ∤ N` is the trace of a pair of
    Weil numbers of weight one.*
    
    ### Why it is TRUE
    
    Eichler–Shimura: the `2g` eigenvalues of Frobenius on `H¹(X_0(N)_{𝔽̄_ℓ})` pair
    up as `αᵢ, βᵢ` with `αᵢ + βᵢ = aᵢ` and `αᵢ βᵢ = ℓ`, the `aᵢ` being the
    eigenvalues of `T_ℓ`; Weil's Riemann hypothesis for the curve `X_0(N)_{𝔽_ℓ}`
    (which has good reduction exactly because `ℓ ∤ N`) gives `|αᵢ| = |βᵢ| = √ℓ`.
    The eigenvalue of an oldform is that of the newform it comes from, at a level
    `M₀ ∣ N` still prime to `ℓ`, so nothing is lost by quantifying over the FULL
    cuspidal space rather than over newforms.
    
    ### The route inside THIS FILE, and the one statement it is missing
    
    Everything below already exists here:
    
    1. `exists_x0Compactification_finiteField N ℓ` — the curve `X_0(N)_{𝔽_ℓ}`;
    2. `exists_isWeilEigenvalues ℓ strX` — its Frobenius eigenvalue multiset `α`,
       pinned by the point counts over every finite extension;
    3. `exists_isCharRootMultiset (heckeOp N ℓ)` — the root multiset `a` of the
       characteristic polynomial of `T_ℓ`, unconditionally;
    4. `isEichlerShimuraTransform_x0 N ℓ … hα ha` — `IsEichlerShimuraTransform ℓ a α`,
       i.e. `α` is a union of pairs summing through `a` with each product `ℓ`,
       possibly together with entries equal to `0`.
    
    The ONLY missing input is **PURITY**:
    
    > for `strX : X ⟶ SpecF ℓ` proper, smooth of relative dimension `1` and
    > geometrically connected, and `IsWeilEigenvalues ℓ strX α`, every `z ∈ α` with
    > `z ≠ 0` has `‖z‖ = Real.sqrt ℓ`.
    
    That is Weil's theorem, it mentions no modular curve, and it is stated exactly
    where `IsWeilEigenvalues` is defined.  It is faithful in that shape because
    `IsWeilEigenvalues` pins `α` only UP TO ENTRIES EQUAL TO ZERO
    (`pow_sum_eq_of_isWeilEigenvalues`), so the `z ≠ 0` guard is required rather
    than defensive; and the entries this leaf consumes are automatically nonzero,
    their product being `ℓ ≠ 0`.  **Check the emptiness convention before writing
    it**: the section docstring on `IsWeilEigenvalues` observes that for an EMPTY
    `X` the counts force `α = {1, ℓ}`, which violates purity — so purity needs
    whatever hypothesis excludes the empty scheme (`GeometricallyConnected` does, if
    it is `ConnectedSpace`-based; otherwise add nonemptiness explicitly).
    
    ### Why this leaf is stated here and not proven there
    
    Declaration order — see the section docstring immediately above.  All four
    ingredients live 10 000 lines below this line, and neither block can move past
    the other, so a prover must first SPLIT this module.  Two further things that
    prover will need, and that are not obstacles to the STATEMENT:
    
    * `cuspForm_finiteDimensional` (in `Modularity/Interface.lean`, downstream) — an
      eigenvalue is a root of the characteristic polynomial only in finite
      dimension, and `IsCharRootMultiset` is deliberately stated without it (its
      junk value is the empty multiset).  Hoisting it is the same class of move as
      the one recorded on `heckeOp_smul_of_isWeightTwoEigenform` above;
    * nothing else.  In particular NO Petersson theory: the reality of `a_p`, which
      the old packaging obtained from self-adjointness of `T_p`, falls out of
      `α β = ℓ` together with `‖α‖ = √ℓ` — see `exists_real_of_weilPair` below.
    
    ### Reconnaissance (PARI/GP, 2026-07-28 — untrusted searcher)
    
    For every level `M ≤ 60` with `S₂(Γ₀(M)) ≠ 0` and every prime `q ≤ 40`, the
    matrix of `T_q` (resp. `U_q` when `q ∣ M`) on the FULL cuspidal space was formed
    and its characteristic polynomial's complex roots examined: at `q ∤ M` all roots
    are REAL with `|a_q| ≤ 2√q`; at `q ∣ M` all roots have `|a_q| ≤ √q`.  **540
    matrices, zero violations.**  The first column is this leaf (the reality it
    observes is the corollary below, not an extra assumption); the second is
    `norm_coeff_le_sqrt_of_dvd_level`.
    
    ### Axes searched, and why each dies
    
    * **The coefficient recursions alone.**  `hecke`/`atkin` constrain only the
      RELATIONS among the `a_n`, never their size.  The level-`0` counterexample
      recorded on `stabilizationRoot_ne_natCast` below is built entirely inside
      those recursions, so no proof using only them can exist.
    * **Radius of convergence.**  `qExpansionSummable` gives
      `limsup ‖a_n‖^{1/n} ≤ 1`, but along the sparse sequence `n = p^k` that is
      vacuous: `‖a_p‖^{k/p^k} → 1` whatever `‖a_p‖` is.
    * **Hecke's trivial bound**, including mathlib's new
      `CuspFormClass.qExpansion_isBigO` (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/ModularForms/Bounds.lean`):
      `a_n = O(n)` in weight two, hence `‖a_p‖ ≤ p + 1` — one epsilon short of what
      `stabilizationRoot_ne_natCast` needs (`‖b_p‖ ≠ p + 1`), and `IsBigO` carries
      an UNSPECIFIED constant while `norm_coeff_le_two_mul_self` needs the explicit
      `2`.  The trivial bound is attained by the weight-two Eisenstein series, so
      the strictness wanted is exactly cuspidality, quantitatively.  It does not
      help the reality clause either, which is orthogonal to every size estimate.
    * **The `EichlerShimuraPackage` of `Modularity/Interface.lean`.**  It does
      supply `Frob_q² − T_q Frob_q + q = 0`, i.e. `α + β = a_q`, `α β = q` — but
      `Interface.lean` IMPORTS this file, and more decisively its coefficients live
      in `AlgebraicClosure ℚ_p` and it asserts nothing archimedean.  What turns that
      characteristic polynomial into a bound is purity, which is this leaf. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **THE COEFFICIENT BOUND AT THE PRIMES DIVIDING THE LEVEL** (sorry leaf, cut
    2026-07-31 out of `realCoeff_norm_le_of_isWeightTwoEigenform`): `‖a_p‖ ≤ √p`
    when `p ∣ M`.
    
    ### Why it is TRUE
    
    Atkin–Lehner together with Deligne.  A normalized common eigenform of
    `S₂(Γ₀(M))` comes from a newform `h` of level `M₀ ∣ M`, and the `U_p`-eigenvalue
    is pinned by a two-case computation on the old space `⟨h(dτ) : d ∣ M/M₀⟩`:
    
    * `p ∣ M₀`: `U_p` is upper triangular in the basis ordered by the `p`-part of
      `d`, with diagonal `(a_p(h), 0, …, 0)`, so its eigenvalues are `a_p(h)` and
      `0`; and `|a_p(h)| = 1` if `p ∥ M₀`, `= 0` if `p² ∣ M₀`, both `≤ √p` since
      `p ≥ 2`.
    * `p ∤ M₀`: `U_p v_0 = a_p(h) v_0 − p v_1` and `U_p v_j = v_{j−1}`, whose
      nonzero eigenvalues are the roots of `X² − a_p(h) X + p`; those have product
      `p` and are complex conjugate because `|a_p(h)| ≤ 2√p`, hence each has
      absolute value exactly `√p`.
    
    The second case is where this leaf consumes Deligne, at the level `M₀` and the
    prime `p ∤ M₀` — i.e. it is `exists_weilPair_of_heckeOp_smul` above at another
    level, not a second archimedean assumption.  What it needs BEYOND that leaf is
    the oldform/newform theory (`IsNewEigenformAt` and the degeneracy maps), which
    is why the two are separate leaves: they share their archimedean input and
    nothing else.
    
    **This clause does NOT assert reality, and must not be strengthened to do so
    gratuitously**: at `p ∣ M₀` with `p ∥ M₀` the `U_p`-eigenvalue is the
    pseudo-eigenvalue of the Atkin–Lehner involution times a root of unity, and
    `|a_p| = 1 ≤ √p` is all that is claimed.  Nothing below needs more.
    
    **`hM` IS LOAD-BEARING — the statement is FALSE at `M = 0`.**  See the FALSITY
    AUDIT on `integrableOn_qSeriesAt` above for why `CuspForm (Gamma0GL 0) 2` is a
    near-empty condition (`Γ₀(0) = ⟨T, −I⟩` has `∞` as its only cusp).  Concretely
    `a n = n`, carried by `∑_{n ≥ 1} n q^n = q/(1 − q)²`, satisfies every field of
    `IsWeightTwoEigenform 0 g a` — `hecke` vacuously, `atkin` because `a` is
    completely multiplicative — and at `M = 0` every `p` divides `M`, so this clause
    would demand `‖a p‖ = p ≤ √p`. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **RELATIVE RIEMANN–ROCH: fppf-locally, a degree-zero class twisted by `d·[o]` is
    EFFECTIVE and splits into `d` sections** (sorry leaf, cut 2026-07-31 out of
    `exists_flatSurj_ajListSum`, which is now PROVEN over it and over
    `relPicEquiv_sheaf_listSum_aj` above).
    
    This is `Sym^d C ↠ Pic^d` — **without needing `Sym^d` to be constructed** — and it
    mentions no group law, no `aj` and no `inj`.  Let `L := P.sheaf p` be the invertible
    sheaf on `C_T` classified by `p`, and put `d := max (2g − 1) 1` for `g` the (locally
    constant) genus of `f` on the image of `T`.  Then `L(d·o)` has fibre degree
    `d ≥ 2g − 1`, so `R¹π_* = 0`, `π_*L(d·o)` is locally free of rank `d − g + 1 > 0`,
    and the projective bundle `ℙ(π_*L(d·o)) ⟶ T` is smooth surjective; its tautological
    section cuts a relative effective divisor of degree `d` in the class.  Splitting that
    divisor into `d` individual sections costs one further étale cover (a smooth
    surjection has sections étale-locally, Stacks 054F, applied on the open locus where
    the divisor is étale over the base — nonempty in every fibre once the residue field
    is enlarged).  A composite of a smooth surjection and an étale surjection is flat and
    surjective, which is all the conclusion asks for.  Literature: Milne, *Jacobian
    Varieties* §§2–5; BLR *Néron Models* 9.4; ACGH ch. IV for `Sym^d C ⟶ Pic^d`.
    
    `QuasiCompact h` comes free from the same objects: the projective bundle is proper
    hence quasi-compact, the étale cover splitting the divisor is finite over the base,
    and a composite of quasi-compact morphisms is quasi-compact.  It is in the conclusion
    because `exists_albaneseFactorisation` descends a MORPHISM along `h` and therefore
    needs `EffectiveEpi h` (`Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Sites/Fpqc.lean`), not merely
    `Epi h`.
    
    **`[CompactSpace T]` IS LOAD-BEARING AND THE STATEMENT IS FALSE WITHOUT IT.**  The
    list `l` has ONE length, and `d` above is bounded only because the genus takes
    finitely many values on a quasi-compact `T`.  Witness: let `S = ⨆_{g ≥ 2} Spec ℚ` and
    `C = ⨆_{g ≥ 2} C_g` with `C_g` a smooth projective curve over `ℚ` of genus `g`, so
    `f` is a smooth proper relative curve with geometrically connected fibres, and take
    `T = J`, `p` the universal point.  For any list of length `n` the composite `h`
    factors set-theoretically through the image of the `n`-fold sum map `C^n ⟶ J`, whose
    restriction over the genus-`g` component has dimension at most `n`; so on any
    component with `g > n` that image is a proper closed subscheme of the `g`-dimensional
    `J_g` and `h` cannot be surjective.
    
    **FALSITY AUDIT — WHY `P` STAYS IN THE STATEMENT, i.e. why this is not stated for an
    arbitrary invertible `L`.**  The obvious `P`-free generalisation
    
    > for every invertible `L` on `C_T` there are `T' ⟶ T` flat surjective quasi-compact
    > and `l` with `φ^*L ⊗ ⊗ᵢ𝒪(−yᵢ) ∼ 𝒪(−o)^{⊗ l.length}`
    
    is **FALSE**, and the counterexample is one line of degree bookkeeping.  Take
    `S = T = Spec k`, `C/k` a smooth projective curve, and `L` of degree `m ≠ 0`.  A
    cover `T' ⟶ T` is a nonempty `k`-scheme; comparing fibre degrees at any point of `T'`
    (the twist `π^*N` in `RelPicEquiv` has fibre degree `0`, being pulled back from the
    base) gives `m − l.length = − l.length`, i.e. `m = 0`.  So the `P`-free statement
    holds precisely for `L` of relative degree `0`.
    
    `P.sheaf p` HAS relative degree `0`, and that is a consequence of the structure
    rather than an extra hypothesis: `deg ∘ P.sheaf` is a locally constant `ℤ`-valued
    function on `T`, additive by `sheaf_add`, zero at the origin by `sheaf_zero`, and
    zero on the Abel–Jacobi image by `aj_spec` (`1 − 1 = 0`); since `jf` has
    geometrically connected fibres it is therefore identically `0`.  There is no degree
    theory at this pin to say any of that formally, which is exactly why the leaf is
    stated for `P.sheaf p` and not for a general `L` — the hypothesis "degree zero" is
    not expressible here, and `P` is the only thing in scope that carries it.
    
    The same audit explains why the two occurrences of `l.length` in the conclusion may
    not be relaxed to two independent numbers.  With an independent `n` the statement is
    true for arbitrary `L` (`𝒪(D' − D)` with `#D' − #D = deg L`), but it no longer
    matches `relPicEquiv_sheaf_listSum_aj`, whose two lengths are equal by construction,
    and the assembly below cannot close — one would have to prove `deg L = 0` on the way,
    which is the theory that does not exist.
    
    **NOT VACUOUS.**  `l = []` forces `P.sheaf p ∼ 𝒪`, hence `p = 0` by `inj` and
    `sheaf_zero`, so the empty list does not discharge the conclusion for a `p` of
    nonzero class; and `T' = ∅` is excluded by `Surjective h` whenever `T` is nonempty. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **ABEL'S THEOREM, with `Pic⁰` REMOVED FROM THE STATEMENT** (sorry leaf, cut
    2026-07-31 out of `IsRelPicZeroOf.listSum_map_eq_of_listSum_aj_eq`, which is now
    PROVEN over it and over `relPicEquiv_divisor_of_listSum_aj_eq` above): the divisor
    map `Σ nᵢ[yᵢ] ↦ Σ nᵢ c(yᵢ)` into an abelian scheme depends only on the relative
    LINEAR EQUIVALENCE class of the divisor.
    
    Equivalently: `Sym^d C ⟶ A` factors through `Pic^d_{C/S}`.  This is the classical
    Abel theorem for a map to an abelian variety, proved by the theorem of the cube /
    the seesaw theorem (Mumford, *Abelian Varieties* §§6 and 11; Milne, *Jacobian
    Varieties* §§2–6; BLR *Néron Models* 8.4).
    
    **WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT CUT.**  The node it replaces took its hypothesis in the
    vocabulary of `IsRelPicZeroOf` — "the two lists have equal `aj`-sums" — which is a
    statement about a representing object for `Pic⁰` that a classical proof never
    mentions.  Here the hypothesis is a linear equivalence of two EFFECTIVE divisors on
    the curve, and the conclusion is about `c` alone.  Nothing in the statement mentions
    `J`, `ab`, `aj`, `sheaf` or `inj`; the only project vocabulary left is
    `RelPicEquiv`, which is `Pic(C_T)/Pic(T)` and is unavoidable — the relative Picard
    group is what "linearly equivalent over a base" MEANS.
    
    **FAITHFULNESS.  `hnat` and `hpt` are both load-bearing, with the same two witnesses
    as on the node this replaces**, transported through
    `relPicEquiv_divisor_of_listSum_aj_eq`, which is an implication and so carries any
    counterexample to the hypothesis side unchanged:
    
    * without `hnat`, take `l = [o_T]`, `l' = []`: the divisors `1·[o] + 0` and
      `0·[o] + [o_T]` are equal, hence certainly equivalent, and the conclusion forces
      `c g o_T = 0`.  A non-natural `c` vanishing at `(𝟙 S, o)` and not at some other
      test object violates exactly that;
    * without `hpt`, the constant family `c g x := τ|_T` for a nonzero `τ` is natural
      and gives `τ_T ≠ 0 = 0` on the same pair.
    
    **THE TWIST `π^*N` IN `RelPicEquiv` IS NOT A WEAKENING OF THE HYPOTHESIS, and this
    is the one check a prover must not skip.**  `RelPicEquiv` allows the two divisor
    sheaves to differ by a sheaf pulled back from `T`, not merely by a principal
    divisor.  That is still enough: a `π^*N` restricts to `𝒪` on every fibre, so the
    difference of the two divisors is fibrewise principal, which is precisely the
    hypothesis of the relative Abel theorem — and it is the RIGHT hypothesis, because
    `Sym^d C ⟶ A` factors through the RELATIVE Picard functor `Pic(C_T)/Pic(T)`, not
    through `Pic(C_T)`.  Weakening `RelPicEquiv` to plain isomorphism here would make
    the leaf strictly weaker than its consumer needs, since
    `relPicEquiv_divisor_of_listSum_aj_eq` can only ever produce a `RelPicEquiv`.
    
    **NOT VACUOUS.**  `l = l'` makes the hypothesis an identity and the conclusion an
    identity; the content is at `l ≠ l'`, and such pairs exist as soon as the curve has
    genus `≥ 1`, because then `Sym^d C ⟶ Pic^d` has positive-dimensional fibres for
    `d ≥ 2g`.  `_hf` is recorded because the classical proof is a statement about
    curves; it is not consumed by the statement. -/

    over `gammaZeroIndex`, `numEllipticTwo`, `numEllipticThree` and `numCusps`.
    Diamond–Shurman Theorem 3.1.1.  This statement mentions NO modular form: it
    relates the scheme handed over by `exists_x0Compactification` to the arithmetic
    of `N`, and nothing else.
    
    **FAITHFULNESS.**  The four counting functions are PARI/GP-validated to
    `N = 1000` against `mfdim([N,2],1)` (1000 of 1000, dimensions to 201; recorded
    on the bridge below), and `12 + μ − 3ν₂ − 4ν₃ − 6ν∞ ≡ 0 (mod 12)` at every `N`

    Treat the four definitions as FIXED and prove against them.
    
    `0 < N` is load-bearing for the same reason it is on the bridge: `x0Genus 0 = 1`
    (not `0` — `Nat.divisors 0 = ∅` makes every ingredient vanish and the formula
    reads `12/12`), so the degenerate level is not harmlessly excluded by the
    arithmetic.
    
    **WHAT IT SAYS ABOUT `g` IS UNAMBIGUOUS.**  `IsCurveGenus.unique` in
    `CurveGenus.lean` pins `g` from `strX` alone, given one closed point of finite
    positive residue degree — which a proper curve over `ℚ` has — so this is a
    statement about `X_0(N)`, not about a choice of witness.
    
    **ROUTE.**  Riemann–Hurwitz applied to the degree-`μ` cover
    `X_0(N) → X_0(1) = ℙ¹`, whose ramification is exactly the elliptic points

    input is the behaviour of `ell` along a finite morphism; the alternative is to
    compute `ell` on `X_0(N)` directly from the `j`-map, which this file already
    carries as `IsJMapOn`.  Neither is short; the first is the one every textbook
    takes. -/

    DedekindEta.lean` at this pin still carries only the ANALYTIC properties of `η`

    transformation law under `SL(2, ℤ)`, no multiplier system, and therefore no
    criterion for an eta quotient to be a modular form of a given level.  So the
    eta-quotient axis is not a short route either at this pin; it would have to
    begin with `η(−1/z) = √(−iz)·η(z)` and the Ligozat conditions.
    
    **WHY THE EXISTENTIAL AND NOT A NAMED MATRIX.**  Because `A` depends on `b`,
    which is universally quantified: a different basis gives a conjugate matrix.
    Fixing `A` would force a choice of basis into the statement, which is exactly
    what the cut on this node deliberately avoided.  The characteristic polynomial
    is the conjugation-invariant part, and that is the part that is banked.
    
    **NOT VACUOUS.**  The hypothesis `b` forces `dim_ℂ S₂(Γ₀(N)) = d`, and eleven
    of the sixteen banked polynomials have a nonzero non-leading coefficient, so
    the second conjunct genuinely pins the operator.  Faithfulness of the banked
    numbers themselves is settled affirmatively at all sixteen rows by the PARI/GP
    runs recorded on `x0HeckeCharpolyTable`. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **GOOD REDUCTION ON A GIT MODEL: some atlas over `ℤ_(ℓ)` has the
    categorical-quotient property at the CLOSED POINT** (sorry leaf, opened
    2026-07-31 — Katz–Mazur (8.1.6), Deligne–Rapoport, Igusa).
    
    This is the special-fibre twin of `exists_gamma0AtlasOver_bcQuotient_of_field`,
    and it replaces `universal_classifyPullback_special`, which is PROVEN over it
    (through `bcUniversal_specLocSpecial` just below).  The leaf count is unchanged;
    what changed is that the statement is now attackable on a model of one's own
    choosing.
    
    ## What it says, unfolded
    
    `Gamma0AtlasOver.BcQuotient q` at `q = SpecLoc.special toF` asks that a morphism
    out of `𝔐 ×_{ℤ_(ℓ)} 𝔽_ℓ` over `𝔽_ℓ`, which does not separate two rigidifications
    of one datum lying over one base point, factor uniquely — over `𝔽_ℓ` — through
    the classifying map of the base-changed universal family.  On a GIT model, where
    `𝔐 = Spec A` and `Y = Spec (A^G)`, that is the assertion
    
        Spec (A^G ⊗_R 𝔽_ℓ)  is the categorical quotient of  Spec (A ⊗_R 𝔽_ℓ)  by  G
    
    in `𝔽_ℓ`-schemes.  Everything ELSE about this base change is already formal:
    `Gamma0AtlasOver.baseChange` builds seven of the eight atlas fields at an
    ARBITRARY base morphism, out of `exists_gamma0Datum_baseChange` and
    `IsBaseChangeOf.cancel`, and `bcQuotient_of_bcUniversal` /
    `bcUniversal_of_bcQuotient` make the quotient and initiality forms equivalent at
    an arbitrary `q`.  So a prover owes exactly the displayed line and nothing else.
    
    ## Why `A` is a hypothesis, and why the conclusion is existential
    
    Both for the reason recorded on `exists_gamma0AtlasOver_bcQuotient_of_field`.
    Without `A` the statement would also assert `Nonempty (Gamma0AtlasOver N (SpecLoc R))`,
    which is a DIFFERENT theorem (`nonempty_gamma0AtlasOver_specLoc`, already proven
    for `0 < N`) and does not belong here.  And a lazy witness `A'` cannot weaken the
    consumer: `bcUniversal_transport` derives the conclusion for the GIVEN atlas from
    whatever `A'` is supplied, through the canonical classify-compatible isomorphism
    of coarse spaces, so no choice of model can make the statement cheaper than it is.
    
    ## Why nothing formal reaches it
    
    `bcUniversal_of_flat` — (8.1.6)(2) at a flat extension of scalars — is what closes
    the generic side (`bcUniversal_specLocGeneric` above) and the field side
    (`bcUniversal_of_field`).  It does NOT reach the closed point, for two independent
    reasons: `ℤ_(ℓ) → 𝔽_ℓ` is not flat, and `ℓ` need not be prime to `#G`
    (`#GL₂(ℤ/3) = 48`, so `ℓ = 2` divides it already at the auxiliary level that
    `exists_gamma0Rigidification_specF` chooses).  So "invariants commute with base
    change" fails here for FORMAL reasons, and Katz–Mazur Remark (8.1.7)'s
    counterexamples at `p = 2, 3` are about precisely this base change.  The content
    has to come from the geometry of the moduli problem over `ℤ[1/N]` — flatness of
    `𝔐` over the base, and reducedness of the special fibre — and not from invariant
    theory alone.  Do not expect a `specInvariants_universal`-shaped proof: that is
    what `Gamma0GITPresentationOver.bcQuot_universal_of_field` is on the field side,
    and its argument is exactly the one that breaks here.
    
    ## DO NOT attack it through the `𝔽_ℓ`-side coarse space — that is CIRCULAR
    
    `exists_isCoarseModuliY0_specInvariants` does give a coarse moduli space
    `Spec (A^G)` for `Γ₀(N)/𝔽_ℓ`, and coarse spaces are pinned up to unique
    isomorphism by initiality — so "compare the special fibre with `Spec (A^G)`" IS
    `isIso_specialFibreInvariantsQuotient`, which is PROVEN over this leaf's consumer.
    `exists_specialFibreInvariantsIso`, `exists_gamma0GITPresentationData_pullbackSpecial`,
    `exists_gamma0AtlasData_pullbackSpecial` and `nonempty_isCoarseModuliY0_pullbackSpecial`
    are all downstream of it for the same reason, so none of them is an input here.
    The `𝔽_ℓ`-side objects that ARE legitimate inputs are the ones built directly in
    characteristic `ℓ` and independent of the integral model:
    `exists_gamma0Rigidification_specF` and `nonempty_gamma0CurveAtlasOver_zmod`.
    
    ## Faithfulness
    
    `_hℓN` is load-bearing for TRUTH, not merely carried: at `ℓ ∣ N` the special fibre
    of `Y_0(N)_{ℤ_(ℓ)}` is the Deligne–Rapoport crossing of two Igusa components and
    is not the coarse space of `Γ₀(N)/𝔽_ℓ` at all, so the quotient property fails.
    `_hbase` is what makes `SpecLoc.special toF` the honest closed point rather than
    an arbitrary ring map to `ZMod ℓ`.  `_hℓ` is carried for the same reason as by
    every neighbour in this cluster.  Note `_hℓN` also forces `0 < N` outright
    (`ℓ ∣ 0` for every `ℓ`), so the degenerate level cannot arise under these
    hypotheses and no `N = 0` branch is owed anywhere below. -/

    base is affine (`isAffine_of_isCoarseModuliY0` is the `ℚ` form of this; the same

       `CurveCompactification.lean` via `Proj` of a graded chart and with no gluing

       is already stated for arbitrary `B B' : CommRingCat`; only

       GENERALISATION of proven code, not new mathematics.
    2. *Normalise.*  `X := i.normalization`, `jZ := i.toNormalization`, which is an
       open immersion by Zariski's Main Theorem (mathlib, free) and dominant by
       construction; `i.fromNormalization` is integral, and FINITE because `ℤ_(ℓ)` is
       a Nagata ring — the generalisation of `isFinite_fromNormalization`, whose own
       docstring already flags that it is stated over a field only for convenience.
       Finite over proper is proper, which gives `isProper`.  Normality of a relative
       normalisation is `isIntegrallyClosed_stalk_normalization`, and integrality of
       `i.normalization` follows from `IsIntegral Y`.
    3. *The cusp locus is finite.*  This is the one genuinely MODULAR step of the
       three, and it is why this leaf is not pure algebraic geometry.  Over a field
       `finite_compl_range_toNormalization` gets it from `topologicalKrullDim ≤ 1` on
       the total space; over a DVR the total space has dimension `2` and that argument
       is unavailable.  What is true, and modular, is that the cusps are FINITE OVER
       THE BASE — a finite scheme over `Spec ℤ_(ℓ)` is a semilocal noetherian ring of
       dimension `≤ 1` and so has finitely many points — and that `Y_0(N)_{𝔽_ℓ}`
       meets every component of the special fibre, i.e. no whole component of
       `X_0(N)_{𝔽_ℓ}` is cuspidal.  Without the second clause the boundary can be a
       whole component and `finite_compl` is FALSE; that is the precise place where a
       "general relative curve over a Dedekind base" statement would break, and it is
       why the leaf is stated for the modular curve rather than for an arbitrary
       affine relative curve.

    the coarse space is empty (`isEmpty_of_isCoarseModuliY0_zero_base`), `XZ` would be
    a finite integral proper scheme, and `IsX0Compactification` — the consumer — would
    be unsatisfiable.  Here it is supplied by `pos_of_isX0Compactification` rather
    than by carrying the `SpecQ`-side hypothesis, so this statement mentions no

    exists over `ℤ[1/N]` and one may base change), and is carried only because both
    consumers hold it and because dropping it would strand the leaf's `_hbase`
    parameter; a prover may ignore it. -/

    a perfect field ⟹ smooth".  **Over a discrete valuation base the same implication
    is FALSE**: the normalisation of a nodal degeneration is normal and is not
    smooth over the base.  So no amount of general algebraic geometry gives this, and
    `ℓ ∤ N` is not decoration — it is the whole hypothesis.  That asymmetry is the
    single sharpest way to see why the integral model is a citation and the rational
    one is not.
    
    **What the hypothesis pins.**  `_M` does not merely supply a scheme; it pins `XZ`
    up to isomorphism over the base (see the subsection comment: integral + normal +
    proper + finite complement, over a fixed dense open, has at most one solution).
    That is what makes a statement about an ARBITRARY model true rather than a
    statement about a chosen one, and it is the assumption a successor should check
    first if the leaf resists.
    
    **The cut a prover should take next**, which is one level finer than this leaf
    and was left undone only for budget: `XZ` is flat over the DVR base (integral,
    dominating a Dedekind base ⟹ torsion-free ⟹ flat), so by the fibrewise criterion
    smoothness reduces to smoothness of the SPECIAL FIBRE `XZ ×_{ℤ_(ℓ)} 𝔽_ℓ` over
    `𝔽_ℓ`, which is Igusa's theorem proper, plus smoothness of the generic fibre,
    which is `_hX`-free general curve theory over `ℚ` and already available in this
    file.  Splitting there converts one citation into one citation and two proofs. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
before anyone tried), but its FALSITY AUDITS as binding until you refute
them yourself.  An absence claim ('not in mathlib', 'nowhere stated') is
scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **THE FORMATION OF `Pic⁰` COMMUTES WITH BASE CHANGE** (sorry leaf,
    opened 2026-07-31 as the replacement for `isIso_jacobianBaseChangeComparison`,
    which is now PROVEN over it, and since then the ONLY one in this block —
    FGA exposé 232; BLR *Néron Models* 8.1, 9.4).
    
    ## What it says
    
    `P` presents `J` as the object representing `Pic⁰_{C/S}`, rigidified along
    `o`; the conclusion is that `J ×_S S'` represents `Pic⁰_{C'/S'}` for the
    fibre, with the Abel–Jacobi map PINNED to the canonical one
    `x ↦ (P.aj of x read down through eX) read up into the fibre product`.
    That last clause is the whole reason the leaf is stated with an `∃` and a
    pinning equation rather than as a bare `Nonempty`: without it the
    conclusion says only that *some* Picard structure exists on `J ×_S S'`,
    which is not enough to identify the comparison morphism below.
    
    ## Why this is a strictly SMALLER citation than the one it replaces
    
    Everything about representability is already spent — `exists_relPicZero`
    is where FGA 232 is cited, and this leaf takes an `IsRelPicZeroOf` as a
    HYPOTHESIS.  What is left is the transport of a classification of
    invertible sheaves across the canonical isomorphism of curves
    `C' ≅ C ×_S S'` (`exists_fibreIdentIso`), and the classical proof of that
    is one sentence: the value of the relative Picard FUNCTOR at `g : T ⟶ S'`
    depends on `T` only through `C' ×_{S'} T`, and `C' ×_{S'} T ≅ C ×_S T`
    because `g` factors through `s`.  So the two functors are equal on the
    nose, not merely isomorphic, and `J ×_S S'` represents the second because
    `J` represents the first.
    
    ## The route, with every ingredient priced (2026-07-31)
    
    Write `ψ g : curveBaseChange f' g ≅ curveBaseChange f (g ≫ s)` for the
    per-test-object isomorphism.  It is `φ` (from `exists_fibreIdentIso`)
    base changed along `g`, followed by
    `Limits.pullbackLeftPullbackSndIso f s g`, and
    `pullbackLeftPullbackSndIso_hom_snd` gives
    `ψ.hom ≫ curveBaseChangeProj f (g ≫ s) = curveBaseChangeProj f' g`,
    which is the compatibility every clause below needs.  Then set
    `P'.sheaf p := modPullback (ψ g).hom (P.sheaf (RelPoint.baseChangeDown s p))`.
    
    | field | what it costs | status of the ingredient |
    |---|---|---|
    | `invertible` | `isInvertibleSheaf_modPullback` | PROVEN |
    | `inj` | `modPullback (ψ.inv)` back, then `P.inj`; needs pseudofunctoriality | `modPullbackCompIso`, `modPullbackCongrIso`, `modPullbackUnitIso` all PROVEN |
    | `sheaf_zero` | `AbelianSchemeStruct.baseChange_zero` + `modPullback` of the unit | PROVEN |
    | `sheaf_add` | `baseChange_add` + `nonempty_modPullback_modTensorPic` | PROVEN |
    | `sheaf_pre` | naturality of `ψ` in `T`, a `pullback.hom_ext` | free |
    | `aj`, `aj_pre`, `aj_base` | `RelPoint.baseChangeUp/Down` bookkeeping | free |
    | `aj_spec` | **`modPullback (ψ g).hom (sectionIdeal (relSection y)) ≅ sectionIdeal (relSection x)`** | **THE ONE NEW GEOMETRIC INPUT** |
    
    So the honest residue is the last row: `𝒪(−σ)` transports across an
    ISOMORPHISM of relative curves matching the sections.  That is the easy
    case of `nonempty_modPullback_sectionIdeal` next door in
    `RelativePicard.lean` — for an isomorphism `modPullback` is exact, so it
    commutes with the kernel that defines `sectionIdeal`, and no flatness of
    the divisor is needed.  A successor should state it there, beside its
    harder sibling, and prove this leaf over it.
    
    **FAITHFULNESS.**  `_ho` is load-bearing for TRUTH, not decoration: the
    `aj_base` field of the conclusion asserts that the pinned Abel–Jacobi map
    sends `o'` to the origin, and that is false unless `o'` corresponds to
    `o` under `eX`.  `_hf` is what makes `C'` a smooth proper curve at all
    (`isSmoothProperCurve_of_fibreIdent`), hence what makes the conclusion a
    statement about a curve rather than about an arbitrary `S'`-scheme.
    NOT VACUOUS: the hypotheses are inhabited at every base by
    `exists_relPicZero` together with `fibreIdentPullback`.
    
    **The check that would refute it**: exhibit a smooth proper relative
    curve with a section, a base change, and an invertible sheaf on the
    fibre's base change that is NOT the transport of one upstairs — i.e. a
    failure of `Pic` to commute with base change for a curve with a section.
    Classically impossible, which is why the classical proof is the one
    sentence above and not an argument.
    
    ## The audit below was written about the leaf this one REPLACED
    
    Everything from here to the end of this docstring was the audit of
    `isIso_jacobianBaseChangeComparison`, which is PROVEN below over the
    statement above.  It is kept verbatim rather than deleted because its
    gate verdicts (coherent pushforward is NOT the gate; representability
    is), its inventory of the sheaf calculus, and its pricing all apply
    unchanged to the statement above — and because the final paragraph is
    where the `1 → 1` route was found.
    
    **`u` IS PINNED, not merely constrained.**  `_hu` and `_huaj` are exactly
    the two clauses of the `∃!` produced by
    `jac'.universal (ab.baseChange s) (jacobianBaseChangeAj s jac eX) …`, so
    any two morphisms satisfying them are equal.  There is no adversarial
    witness to pin further: an automorphism of `J ×_S S'` post-composed onto
    `u` breaks `_huaj` unless it fixes every Abel–Jacobi point, and the
    uniqueness clause of `jac'.universal` says that forces it to be `𝟙`.
    
    **NOT VACUOUS.**  Such a `u` exists, because `jacobianBaseChangeAj` is
    natural (`jacobianBaseChangeAj_pre`) and pointed
    (`jacobianBaseChangeAj_base`), which is precisely the input
    `jac'.universal` asks for; and `jac'` itself exists, by
    `exists_relativeJacobian` applied to `f'` through
    `isSmoothProperCurve_of_fibreIdent`.  That is how the two consumers below
    discharge the hypotheses, so this leaf is reachable rather than
    decorative.
    
    **`_hf` and `_ho` are load-bearing even though neither is used to state
    `u`.**  `_ho` is what makes `jacobianBaseChangeAj` POINTED, hence what
    makes a `u` satisfying `_hu` and `_huaj` exist at all; drop it and the
    hypotheses are no longer known to be satisfiable, so the statement
    degenerates towards vacuity rather than becoming false.  `_hf` is the
    geometry: without it `J` has no reason to be a Jacobian in the sense the
    classical theorem is about, and the base change of an Albanese of a
    non-curve is not an Albanese.
    
    **The classical proof.**  `Pic⁰_{C/S}` represents the relative Picard
    functor rigidified along `o`, and the formation of that FUNCTOR commutes
    with base change BY INSPECTION: its value at `T ⟶ S` depends on `T` only
    through `C ×_S T`, and `C ×_S T = C' ×_{S'} T` whenever `T ⟶ S` factors
    through `S'`.  So `J'` and `J ×_S S'` represent the same functor on
    `Sch/S'`, `u` is the induced map of representing objects, and Yoneda
    makes it invertible.  Every step of that is representability; none of it
    is `f_*𝒪_X = 𝒪_S`.

scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **`w_N` EXCHANGES THE CUSPS ABOVE `d` AND `N/d`** (sorry leaf, new
    2026-07-31) — the Deligne–Rapoport boundary combinatorics of the
    Atkin–Lehner involution, LEVEL-GENERIC, and the single leaf that
    `noFixedRationalCusp_atkinLehner_x0OneSixtyNine` and
    `noFixedRationalCusp_atkinLehner_chabautySemiprimeLevel` now both run over.
    
    TRUE, and classical: Ogg, *Hyperelliptic modular curves*, Bull. SMF **102**
    (1974) §2; Atkin–Lehner, Math. Ann. **185** (1970) §2; Deligne–Rapoport
    VI.6.  The cusps of `X_0(N)` are indexed by pairs `(d, a)` with `d ∣ N` and
    `a ∈ (ℤ/gcd(d, N/d))ˣ`, the cusp `a/c` with `gcd(c, N) = d` lying above
    `d`, and `w_N` carries it to a cusp above `N/d`.  `w` is a homeomorphism
    (`w ≫ w = 𝟙 X`) and the cusps above a single `d` form ONE closed point of
    `X` over `ℚ` — its residue field is `ℚ(ζ_{gcd(d, N/d)})`, which is what
    `degree` records — so the image of that point IS the point above `N/d`, an
    equality of subsets rather than an inclusion.
    
    Written `d * d' = N` rather than `d' = N / d` only to keep the division out
    of the statement; for `d ∈ N.divisors` and `N ≠ 0` the two say the same
    thing, and the consumer supplies `d' = N / d`.
    
    **Why `∃ C` and not `∀ C`, which would be FALSE.**  See the subsection note
    just above: `CuspLocus` does not pin its own indexing tightly enough to
    forbid a relabelling, and a relabelled locus does not satisfy `d ↦ N/d`.
    The existential is the faithful form, and it is also what the literature
    actually provides — the classical indexing, together with the action on it.
    
    **What it will cost to close, and where it overlaps what is already open.**
    This leaf subsumes `Nonempty hX.CuspLocus`, which is proven above
    (`nonempty_cuspLocus`) over the single Deligne–Rapoport leaf
    `exists_cuspResidueIndexing`.  So the intended repair is not a fresh
    construction but a strengthening: carry the Atkin–Lehner action as a
    further conjunct of `exists_cuspResidueIndexing` — whose `gcd(d, N/d)`
    residue computation is the same boundary bookkeeping — and thread it
    through `nonempty_cuspLocus_of_residueIndexing`.  Closed that way this leaf
    disappears instead of being paid for twice; closed as a free-standing
    construction it would duplicate the cusp locus.
    
    **The check that refutes it**: a rational cusp of some `X_0(N)` fixed by
    `w_N` at a level where `d * d ≠ N` for every `d ∈ rationalCuspDivisors N`.
    
    **`_hal` may not be dropped**: `w := 𝟙 X` is an involution over `ℚ` fixing
    every cusp, so without the moduli pin the conclusion fails at every `N`
    having a divisor `d ≠ N / d`. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written

scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **A `Γ₀(169)`-datum over `ℚ̄` that is `169`-isogenous TO ITSELF has an
    IRRATIONAL `j`-invariant** (sorry leaf, cut 2026-07-31 out of
    `not_rationalModuli_of_selfIsogenous_oneSixtyNine`, which is PROVEN over it
    and over nothing else) — the classical Fricke fixed-point count, and the
    whole arithmetic content of this cluster.
    
    **The leaf count is unchanged, one before and one after**, and what changes
    is again the surface area.  The parent still mentioned the coarse moduli
    space, the classifying map, a `ℚ`-rational point, the Galois action and a
    second datum `d'`; this leaf mentions ONE datum, ONE hypothesis and a
    Weierstrass model over `ℚ̄`.  `IsNIsogenyPair 169 d d` says in this file's
    own vocabulary exactly what the classical argument starts from: an isogeny
    `ψ : E ⟶ E` with `ker ψ = C` cyclic of order `169`, a dual `ψ̂` with
    `ker ψ̂ = C` as well, and `ψ̂ψ = ψψ̂ = [169]`.
    
    **WHY `d'` COULD GO, AND IT IS ONE LINE THAT WAS ALREADY PROVEN.**
    `IsNIsogenyPair.baseChange hpair (IsBaseChangeOf.refl d) hiso` transports the
    pair `(d, d')` along the isomorphism `hiso : IsBaseChangeOf (𝟙 _) d d'` and
    returns `IsNIsogenyPair 169 d d`.  So "isogenous to its own `169`-isogeny
    quotient" and "`169`-isogenous to itself" are the SAME hypothesis here, and
    the second is the one a prover can work with.  Nothing about the level
    structure is lost on the way: `IsBaseChangeOf.liesIn_iff` is exactly what
    `baseChange` spends to transport `ker_map` and `ker_dual`.
    
    TRUE, by the CM count, and the argument is the classical one for the fixed
    points of the Fricke involution.
    
    **Step 1 — the hypothesis says exactly `End(E) ∋ ψ` with `ψ² = −169`.**
    `ker_map` and `ker_dual` give `ker ψ = C = ker ψ̂`, and `dual_map` gives
    `ψ̂ψ = [169]`.  Equal kernels force `ψ̂ = uψ` for a unit `u` of `End(E)`, and
    then `uψ² = ψ̂ψ = [169]`.
    
    **Step 2 — the loophole, and why it is closed by CYCLICITY rather than by a
    class number.**  `u = 1` gives `ψ² = 169`, so `ψ = ±13 ∈ ℤ` and
    `ker ψ = E[13] ≅ (ℤ/13)²`, which is NOT cyclic of order `169`; the extra
    units `u = ±i` exist only for `j = 1728` and force `tr(ψ)² = 2 · 169 = 338`,
    not a square (`18² = 324 < 338 < 361 = 19²`); `u` a primitive sixth root
    exists only for `j = 0` and forces `tr(ψ)² = 169`, i.e. `ψ = ±13ζ` with
    `ker ψ = E[13]` again.  So `u = −1` and `tr ψ = 0`, i.e. **`ψ² = −169`,
    `ψ = ±13i`** — there is no other square root of `−169` in any imaginary
    quadratic field.  The order `O = End(E)` therefore contains `ℤ[13i]`, and
    the only orders of `ℚ(i)` doing so are `ℤ[13i]` and the maximal order
    `ℤ[i]`.  The maximal order is excluded by the LEVEL STRUCTURE, before any
    arithmetic: `i` is a unit there, so `ker(13i) = E[13] ≅ (ℤ/13)²` is not
    cyclic, whereas `ℤ[13i]/(13i) ≅ ℤ/169` is.  This is also why the classical
    Fricke count of the fixed points of `w_N` is `h(−4N)` (plus `h(−N)` when
    `N ≡ 3 mod 4`) and NOT a sum over every order containing `ℤ[√−N]`;
    `169 ≡ 1 (mod 4)`, so only the first family occurs here.
    
    **Step 3 — the count.**  `disc ℤ[13i] = −676`, and `h(−676) = 6`
    (`quadclassunit(-676).no`, PARI/GP 2.17.4, re-run 2026-07-31).  The class
    polynomial `polclass(-676)` has degree `6` and is IRREDUCIBLE over `ℚ`
    (`polisirreducible`, same session), so `j(E)` has degree exactly `6` over
    `ℚ` and in particular is not rational.  A `ℚ`-rational moduli point of `d`
    would make `j(E)` the value of the `j`-map at a `ℚ`-point, hence rational.
    Contradiction.
    
    **The check that refutes it**: `h(−676) = 1`, or — equivalently and more
    directly checkable in Lean — a rational root of `polclass(-676)`.
    
    **NOT VACUOUS, and this is worth stating because the far consumer IS
    vacuous.**  The hypotheses are satisfiable: there are exactly `6` data over
    `ℚ̄` meeting them, the CM points of discriminant `−676`.  So the content of
    this leaf is the irrationality of their `j`-invariants and nothing weaker.
    (`noFixedModuliPoint_atkinLehner_x0OneSixtyNine`, two nodes down, is
    vacuously true because `Y_0(169)(ℚ) = ∅` — but that is Kenku's theorem, it
    is proved BELOW and only through this node, and the circularity note further
    up is why neither this leaf nor its parent may go near it.)
    
    **What proving it needs**: the theory of complex multiplication —
    `End(E)` for a CM elliptic curve as an order in an imaginary quadratic
    field, and the degree of the class polynomial.  Absent from mathlib as of
    this pin: `grep -rn "classPolynomial\|HilbertClassPolynomial\|ringClassField"
    .lake/packages/mathlib/` returns nothing.
    
    **THE NEXT CUT, if this is still too large for one owner**, is at the
    Weierstrass model: turn `_hself` into an ENDOMORPHISM of `W` in the
    vocabulary of `EllipticCurve/Isogeny.lean` (an `IsRationalMap` certificate
    plus additivity), and leave *"an elliptic curve over `ℚ̄` carrying an
    endomorphism `ψ` with `ψ² = [−169]` whose kernel is cyclic of order `169`
    has `j ∉ ℚ`"* as the arithmetic residue — a statement with no scheme in it
    at all.  It was NOT taken here because the bridge `IsWeierstrassModel` →
    `IsRationalMap` does not exist in this tree and is itself a real piece of
    work, whereas the cut recorded above cost two lines. -/

A refutation with an explicit counterexample is a FULL SUCCESS, not a failure --
say so plainly and restate the leaf correctly.  So is discovering the leaf is
already proven elsewhere, or that it is DEAD (no consumer reaches it): grep the

moves the count and not the project.

treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written

scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **THE NORM-ENDOMORPHISM PAIR OF A SURJECTION** (sorry leaf, new
    2026-07-31) — the whole of what is left of
    `exists_finiteKernelComplement_of_surjective_isAdditiveOn` below, which is
    PROVEN over it.
    
    **WHAT IT ASKS FOR.**  Two endomorphisms `c, d` of `J` over `ℚ`, with `c`
    additive, such that
    
    * `c ≫ d = 0` — the image of `c` lies in `ker d`;
    * `c ≫ u` is surjective — the image of `c` still maps ONTO `A`;
    * `ker d ∩ ker u` is a FINITE `ℚ`-scheme (`IsFinite` of the structure morphism
      of `ker (ker d ↪ J ≫ u)`).
    
    `d` is not required to be additive and no idempotence equation is imposed:
    those are properties of the intended construction, not of what the consumer
    spends.  The three clauses above are exactly what the proof below consumes,
    and nothing else.
    
    **WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT CUT.**  The leaf below had to produce an abelian
    scheme `B` and a map INTO `J` — i.e. an abelian SUBvariety — which is why the
    "image, not quotient" substitution that closed `exists_involutionSignSplitting`
    does not apply to it (that structure only ever needs maps OUT of `A`).  This
    leaf produces no scheme at all: it is a statement about `End(J)` and two
    kernels.  `B` is then `Im c`, delivered by
    `exists_addSurjectiveAbelianImage_of_isAdditiveOn_aux` above, and every
    scheme-theoretic step below — surjectivity, the factorisation through `ker d`,
    and the finiteness transport — is proven.
    
    TRUE and classical (Mumford, *Abelian Varieties* §19 Thm 1; Milne, *Abelian
    Varieties* I.12.1).  Over a field of characteristic `0` put `Y := (ker u)⁰`,
    fix a polarisation `λ` of `J`, and let `N_Y ∈ End J` be the NORM
    ENDOMORPHISM of `Y` — `N_Y = ι_Y ∘ ψ ∘ ι_Y^∨ ∘ λ` with `ψ` the
    `deg λ|_Y`-multiple inverse of `λ|_Y`, so that `N_Y ∘ N_Y = n · N_Y` for an
    `n ≥ 1` and `Im N_Y = Y`.  Take
    
        d := N_Y,        c := [n] − N_Y.
    
    Then `c ≫ d = n·N_Y − N_Y² = 0`; `c ≫ u = [n] ∘ u` because `N_Y` factors
    through `Y ⊆ ker u`, and `[n]` is surjective (`surjective_mulByNat`), so
    `c ≫ u` is surjective; and `ker N_Y ∩ ker u` is killed by `n·m`, where `m` is
    the order of the finite group `(ker u)/Y` — for `x` in it, `m·x ∈ Y ∩ ker N_Y`,
    and `N_Y` acts on `Y` as `[n]` — hence finite.
    
    **DEGENERATE CASES CHECK OUT.**  If `u` is already an isogeny then `Y = 0`,
    `N_Y = 0`, and the pair is `c = 𝟙`, `d = 0`: the last clause reads "`ker u` is
    finite", which is what "isogeny" means.  If `A` is a point then `Y = J`,
    `N_Y = [n]`, `c = 0` and `d = [n]`: `c ≫ u` is the structure morphism of a
    nonempty scheme onto `Spec ℚ`, surjective, and `ker [n] ∩ ker u = J[n]` is
    finite.
    
    **FALSITY AUDIT — NOT JUNK-SATISFIABLE, and the finiteness clause is where
    all the content sits.**  This is a FIRST audit: the statement is new and
    inherits none.
    
    * `c := 𝟙`, `d := 0` (the zero endomorphism `jstr ≫ zeroSection`) satisfies
      `c ≫ d = 0` and `Surjective (c ≫ u) = Surjective u`, and FAILS the third
      clause except when `u` is itself an isogeny: `ker 0 = J`, so the clause
      becomes `IsFinite (ker u ⟶ Spec ℚ)`.  `J = A × A'` with `u = pr₁` and
      `A' ≠ 0` refutes it.
    * `c := 0` fails `Surjective (c ≫ u)` unless `A` is a point: the image of
      `jstr ≫ abA.zeroSection` is the single point `e ∈ A`.
    * `d := 𝟙` forces `c = c ≫ 𝟙 = 0`, i.e. the previous case.
    
    **`_hsurj` MAY NOT BE DROPPED.**  Witness `J := E`, `A := E × E`,
    `u := (𝟙, 0)` — the parent's witness, and it kills this statement for the same
    reason: `c ≫ u : E ⟶ E × E` factors through a curve, so it is never surjective
    onto a surface, whatever `c` is.
    
    **`_hadd` IS NOT LOAD-BEARING FOR TRUTH** (same as the parent, and by the same
    rigidity argument: every morphism of abelian varieties is a translate
    `t_P ∘ h` of a homomorphism, `Surjective (c ≫ t_P ∘ h) = Surjective (c ≫ h)`,
    and `ker (t_P ∘ h) = h^{-1}(−P)` is a torsor under `ker h`, so
    `ker d ∩ ker (t_P ∘ h)` is empty or a torsor under `ker d ∩ ker h`, finite
    either way).  It is kept because the intended construction needs `(ker u)⁰` to
    be a subGROUP, and because every consumer has it for free
    (`IsHeckeIsotypicDecomposition.u_add`).
    
    **WHAT PROVING IT NEEDS**, and this is the honest shrunken list: a
    scheme-level POLARISATION of `J` and the NORM ENDOMORPHISM built from it.  The
    "connected component of a closed subgroup scheme" that the parent's old
    estimate named is no longer on the list as stated — `(ker u)⁰` enters only
    through the construction of `N_Y`, and a prover free to choose `d` may take
    any endomorphism whose kernel meets `ker u` finitely.  `PolarizationStruct` in
    `Fermat/FLT/Modularity/TateModule.lean` does NOT discharge this: it is a
    Tate-module-level datum with a level guard and its own docstring carries a
    "DO NOT BUILD ON `PolarizationStruct` AS IT STANDS" note.  There is no dual
    abelian variety in the tree either — `IsRelPicZeroOf` is `Pic⁰` of a CURVE,
    not of an abelian scheme.
    
    **THE CONSUMERS ALL HAVE A JACOBIAN, should a prover want to thread one.**
    Checked declaration by declaration on 2026-07-31, because the cut opposite —
    thread `IsJacobianOf` down the chain and build `((ker u)⁰)^⊥` against the
    canonical principal polarisation — dies immediately if any consumer lacks it.
    All three named on `exists_quasiSection_of_surjective_isAdditiveOn`'s docstring
    hold one: `exists_quasiSection_heckeIsotypicFactor` (explicit binder
    `jac : IsJacobianOf strX ab o`), `isTorsion_factor_of_heckeIsotypic` (the same
    datum as an IMPLICIT binder `{jac}`, forced by its `D :
    IsHeckeIsotypicDecomposition N h jac`) and
    `exists_atkinLehnerDescent_of_factorwise` (explicit binder `jac`).  Note only
    the first is an actual call site today; the other two are the intended ones its
    docstring records.  So the
    "principally polarised because it is a Jacobian" route is OPEN and costs no
    consumer.  It is not taken here because it is not needed: the cut below is
    generic, changes no signature, and threading a hypothesis through four
    theorems is exactly the interface split that CLAUDE.md's seventh invisibility
    class is about. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written

scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **THE `169` HEAD SUM IS BOUNDED BELOW ON THE ATKIN–LEHNER-MINUS
    EIGENSPACE** (sorry leaf, 2026-07-31) — the certified-basis gate of the
    level-`169` numerics, and after the head/tail cut the only thing at `169`
    that a certified basis is needed for.
    
    `∑_{m=1}^{12} a_m · (13/(2πm)) e^{-2πm/13}` is a sum of TWELVE terms.
    Everything past `m = 12` is `norm_tsum_tail_le_oneSixtyNine`, which needs no
    arithmetic input; `1/2 − 1/10 > 0` is the whole of the consumer's proof.
    
    ## What a certified basis has to provide, exactly
    
    `S₂(Γ₀(169))` is `8`-dimensional and entirely NEW — the "no oldforms" half is
    `not_isWeightTwoEigenform_of_properDivisor_oneSixtyNine`, already PROVEN in
    this file, and `mfdim([169,2],1) = mfdim([169,2],0) = 8`.  The eight
    normalized eigenforms fall into three Galois orbits:
    
    | orbit | dim | coefficient field | `w₁₆₉` |
    | ----- | --- | ----------------- | ------ |
    | 1 | 2 | `ℚ[y]/(y² − 3)` | `−1` |
    | 2 | 3 | `ℚ[y]/(y³ − y² − 2y + 1)` | `−1` |
    | 3 | 3 | `ℚ[y]/(y³ − y² − 2y + 1)` | `+1` |
    
    so the minus eigenspace carries exactly FIVE normalized eigenforms.  Their
    first twelve coefficients, as elements of the coefficient field (`a₁ = 1`
    throughout, and `a₁₃ = 0` because `13² ∣ 169`), are
    
    * **orbit 1**, `y² = 3`:
      `a₂ = y`, `a₃ = 2`, `a₄ = 1`, `a₅ = −y`, `a₆ = 2y`, `a₇ = 0`, `a₈ = −y`,
      `a₉ = 1`, `a₁₀ = −3`, `a₁₁ = 0`, `a₁₂ = 2`;
    * **orbit 2**, `y³ = y² + 2y − 1`:
      `a₂ = 1 − y`, `a₃ = 1 − y²`, `a₄ = y² − 2y − 1`, `a₅ = 3 − y²`, `a₆ = y`,
      `a₇ = −y² + 2y + 2`, `a₈ = 2y² − y − 2`, `a₉ = y² + y − 3`, `a₁₀ = 2 − y`,
      `a₁₁ = y² + 1`, `a₁₂ = y² + y − 2`.
    
    Over the five real embeddings the head sum takes the values
    
        0.99955268…   2.34743108…   2.31927908…   1.62128634…   0.57062456…
    
    whose minimum exceeds `1/2` by `14%`.  Both constants therefore have room:
    `1/2` by `14%` and the companion `1/10` by a factor of five.
    
    ## FALSITY AUDIT
    
    TRUE.  Recomputed from scratch in PARI/GP on 2026-07-31 (`mfinit([169,2],0)`,
    `mfeigenbasis`, `mfatkineigenvalues`, `mfembed`) and cross-checked against the
    five `L`-values the consumer's docstring records.  The check is not a
    restatement: `cuspPeriod_eq_one_sub_mul_integral_Ioi_one` at `ε = −1` reads
    `L(f,1) = (4π/√169) · S` with `S` the sum above, and `(4π/13)` times the five
    head-plus-tail values `0.99973352…, 2.34689385…, 2.31818822…, 1.62184972…,
    0.57039842…` is `0.966386…, 2.268610…, 2.240862…, 1.567751…, 0.551372…` — the
    five values named there, to every digit printed.  Two computations sharing no
    step past `mfinit` agree.
    
    **`_hw` IS NOT LOAD-BEARING FOR THIS STATEMENT, and the docstrings that say it
    is are wrong.**  They are corrected rather than deleted because the mistake is
    instructive: the claim is CORRECT at
    `cuspPeriod_ne_zero_atkinLehnerMinus_oneSixtyNine`, where the hypothesis
    really is what makes the period nonzero, and it was carried down onto the
    leaves cut out of that theorem, where it is not.  Orbit `3` (`w₁₆₉ = +1`) has
    `L(f,1) = 0`, but that is the `(1 − ε)` factor vanishing, not the integral:
    orbit `3`'s three embeddings have head sums `0.92672614…`, `1.06730311…`,
    `0.50247365…` — all above `1/2` — and full sums `0.92699184…`,
    `1.06707581…`, `0.50157757…`.  So the statement with `_hw` deleted is also
    TRUE, and orbit `3` is not a counterexample to anything here; a vanishing
    `L`-value refutes nothing about this integral.
    
    `_hw` is nevertheless KEPT.  Proving the bound for five forms is strictly less
    work than for eight, the consumer supplies the hypothesis for free, and a
    leaf should not be stated wider than any call site needs.  The generalisation
    to all eight normalized eigenforms is recorded here as a fact, not asserted as
    a declaration.
    
    `_hf` genuinely may not be dropped: it is what makes `a` the coefficient
    sequence of `f` rather than an arbitrary sequence, and `_hw` constrains `f`
    alone.  **The check that refutes this leaf**: any embedding of orbit `1` or
    `2` whose first twelve coefficients give a head sum below `1/2`, or
    `mfatkineigenvalues(mfinit([169,2],0), 169)` returning a pattern other than
    `[[-1,-1], [-1,-1,-1], [1,1,1]]`. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written

scoped to the import cone and the DATE it was written -- re-grep it:

    /-- **A SMOOTH PROPER GEOMETRICALLY CONNECTED CURVE OVER `Spec ℚ` IS AN
    INTEGRAL SCHEME** (sorry leaf, 2026-07-31) — the residual obligation that
    `RelativePicard.lean`'s `𝒦_X` section names and deliberately does not
    discharge, because it is a statement about the CURVE hypotheses and belongs
    next to them.
    
    TRUE and standard, in three steps, none of which needs anything not at this pin:
    
    * `_hcurve` makes `X ×_ℚ Spec ℚ ⟶ Spec ℚ` smooth, and a scheme smooth over a
      field is REGULAR, hence REDUCED;
    * `_hconn` makes it connected, and a connected reduced scheme all of whose
      local rings are domains (regular local rings are) is IRREDUCIBLE;
    * irreducible + reduced is `IsIntegral`, and nonemptiness comes with
      `GeometricallyConnected` (a connected scheme is nonempty in mathlib's
      convention; if the local convention allows `∅`, the empty curve satisfies
      every hypothesis and this leaf is FALSE for it — see the audit below).
    
    **FAITHFULNESS.  Both curve hypotheses are load-bearing.**
    
    * Drop `_hcurve` and it is FALSE: `Spec ℚ[ε]/(ε²)` over `Spec ℚ` is proper and
      connected but not reduced, so not integral.
    * Drop `_hconn` and it is FALSE: `Spec ℚ ⊔ Spec ℚ` is smooth proper of relative
      dimension `0` — and at relative dimension `1`, two disjoint copies of a
      smooth proper curve — which is reduced but not irreducible.
    * `_hproper` is NOT load-bearing for integrality (`𝔸¹_ℚ` is integral and not
      proper) and is carried only because every consumer already holds it; a prover
      may leave it unused.
    
    **THE ONE THING TO CHECK BEFORE PROVING IT**: whether
    `GeometricallyConnected` as defined in this development forbids the EMPTY
    scheme.  If it does not, the statement must gain a nonemptiness hypothesis —
    which every consumer has, since `RelPoint strX (𝟙 SpecQ)` is inhabited there.
    That is a restatement, not a refutation, and it is a FULL success to report. -/

ITS OWN DOCSTRING, quoted so you can judge before opening the file --
treat its ROUTE and its cost estimates as HYPOTHESES (they were written
